 Acknowledgments

 This report is based upon work supported by the U.S. Department
 of Energy (DOE), Hydrocarbons and Geothermal Energy Office,
 Geothermal Technologies Office, under Contract No. DE-AC36-
 08GO28308 with the National Laboratory of the Rockies. The views
 expressed herein do not necessarily represent the views of the DOE or
 the U.S. Government.

 The authors acknowledge the technical contributions, reviews,
 and strategic guidance of Sean Porse, Jennifer Livermore, Richard
 Potter, Coryne Tasca, and countless others at the U.S. Department
 of Energy Geothermal Technologies Office. We also appreciate our
 colleagues Amanda Kolker, Clayton Pokorny, Koenraad Beckers (now
 with Resfrac), Kristen Lu, Sertac Akar, Jabs Aljubran, Angel McCoy,
 and Ligia E.P. Smith for their reviews of this report. We acknowledge
 the expert contributions, time, and reviews of Jeff Hammond
 (International Ground Source Heat Pump Association, IGSPHA),
 Andrew Stumpf (Illinois State Geological Survey), Zeyneb Magavi
 (HEET), Dev Millstein (Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory), and
 Ryan Dougherty (Geothermal Exchange Organization). We appreciate
 Kelly MacGregor, Dominique Barnes, and Deanna Cook (National
 Laboratory of the Rockies) for their invaluable assistance with editing
 the initial manuscript and developing the final report publication.
 We acknowledge Billy Roberts’ (National Laboratory of the Rockies)
 support in creating some of the detailed maps in this report. We also
 thank the various state agencies, geothermal operators, geothermal
 developers, contractors, and others who have provided data for the

 8
 Firm power refers to sources that are “always on,” like geothermal, and can provide power whenever they are needed. This is in contrast to intermittent
 resources, like wind or solar, which are dependent on weather or time of day.
 9
 Sixteen PPAs totaling 888 MWe, have been signed in California since the 2021 procurement order. NLR researchers found association between ten of
 these PPAs since the 2021 procurement order, resulting in the promise of at least 616 MWe.
 10
 “Identified” hydrothermal resources are known geothermal resource areas where heat, water, and permeability are available. “Undiscovered”
 hydrothermal resources are potential locations where heat, water, and permeability may exist, estimated via GIS-based statistical modeling or other
9 data-driven modeling techniques. 10

 by a company or individual. For competitive leasing, the highest qualifying bid receives the leasing rights. Noncompetitive leases include lands that

In 2024, GTO published a set of GHP case studies34 to help people better understand GHP systems, installations, and benefits. These 19 studies As a part of the Inflation Reduction Act’s Investment Tax Credit, geothermal direct-use projects may be eligible for a base tax credit of 30% if they
detail GHP installations in climate zones across the United States, with varying system types, sizes, and end uses. The results provide real-life were built between 2021 and 2025 (26 USC § 48E; IRS, 2025a).
examples of GHP systems in different parts of the country, making it easier for people to understand how such a system might work for them.

5. Market Drivers

 While recent capacity gains have been modest,
 there are reasons to believe that the geothermal
 market could experience rapid growth in the near
 term. Market growth could result from anticipated
 improvements to both mature and emerging
 technologies, as well as emerging value-added
 uses (e.g., lithium extraction). The expected load Photo from Getty sb10067330b-001
